22. Data Integrity–Related Consults (Office of
Scientific Investigations, Other
Inspections)
A clinical inspection summary was completed by Dr. Anthony Orencia in the Office of Scientific
Investigations, final signature date March 2, 2023. Two foreign clinical study sites and the
Applicant’s site were selected for evaluation as these sites had the highest patient enrollment
(Krakow n = 5 patients and Zagreb n = 4 patients) and overall, a low proportion of patients were
enrolled in the US (6/118 patients (5.1%)). Enrollment in other countries in the European Union
was higher (e.g., Ukraine 10/118 patients (8.5%)), however due to the ongoing war in Ukraine
inspections in these countries were not conducted.
Dr. Orencia states in the review regarding the study site of Dr. Joanna Zdziarska/ Site 480 (ul.
Mikolaja Kopernika 17, Krakow, Malopolskie, 31-501, Poland) that a total of six study subjects
were screened (one subject was a rescreen); five study subjects were enrolled. Three of the five
enrolled subjects completed the study. Two subjects who discontinued were transferred to
alternative therapies during the study pause. The study was ongoing at the time of the inspection.
Regulatory and source documents were reviewed. The audit involved a review of records and
procedures related to the clinical study protocol and its amendments; subject selection criteria
and consenting; test article controls, including accountability; source data evaluation, including
primary efficacy endpoint of bleeding events; adverse event reporting, including assessment of
277
Integrated Review Template, version 3.0 (05/25/2022)
[To complete this review, detailed instructions are available in the Integrated Review Template How-to Guide.]
Reference ID: 5162849

BLA 761315

Concizumab

under-reporting of serious adverse events, and laboratory testing. Source records for all enrolled
study subjects were examined and verifiable. Records were noted to be complete, legible, and
organized. Study activities were conducted in compliance with the protocol. The primary
efficacy endpoint was verifiable. There was no evidence of under-reporting of adverse events
(e.g., no lapses in approvals or failure to file required reports) were noted) and no discrepancies
were noted. No significant objectionable findings were found during the inspection. A list of
inspectional observations (Form FDA 483) was not issued by FDA at the close-out of the
inspection.
Dr. Orencia states in his review regarding the study site of Dr. Ana Boban/Site 385
(Kispaticeva 12, University Hospital Centre, Zagreb 10000 HRV, Croatia) that five study
subjects were screened; four study subjects were enrolled and randomized. Three subjects
discontinued (withdrew from the study). A single subject was still on treatment during the
inspection. Ethics committee approvals, study correspondence, drug accountability, facility
adequacy, staff qualifications, and monitoring procedures were reviewed. This inspection
covered the safety of study subjects along with serious adverse event reporting, protocol
deviations, subject eligibility, overall protocol compliance, and the verification of source
documentation related to study endpoint criteria. Adverse event and serious adverse reporting
appeared adequate and consistent with the study protocol requirements and the case report form
reports. The primary efficacy endpoint (bleeding events) was verifiable. There was no evidence
of under-reporting of adverse events (e.g., no lapses in approvals or failure to file required
reports were noted). No list of inspectional observations (FDA Form 483) was issued by FDA at
the close-out of the site inspection.
Dr. Orencia states in his review regarding the study site of Novo Nordisk Inc./Applicant
(800 Scudders Mill Rd., Plainsboro, NJ 08536) that the Applicant inspection involved
comprehensive onsite review of study monitor selection, site and clinical investigator selection,
clinical site monitoring, form FDA 1572s, financial disclosures, safety reporting and handling,
data monitoring committee activities, study documents, standard operating procedures, data
collection and handling, protocol deviations, and investigational product disposition. Monitoring
actions taken for those clinical investigators who did not comply with the investigational plan
appeared to be adequate. Procedure documents for Novo Nordisk’s review and processing of
serious adverse events (SAEs), such as (a) Q040018 Handling Individual Safety Cases in Argus,
(b) Q140826 NNI Product Safety GxP Instructions, and (c) Q148963 NNI Electronic Submission
of Expedited Safety Reports were assessed during the inspections. FDA’s inspection also
assessed safety oversight performed by medical monitors and safety advisors in addition to the
data monitoring committee (DMC). No discrepancies were observed for adverse event reporting.
At the end of the Applicant inspection, no FDA Form 483 was issued. However, some of the
following relevant discussion items at the close out meeting with Applicant deserve mention: (a)
Ensure adequate oversight of drug product disposition at clinical sites to track and to evaluate
investigative drug products, for example, with temperature deviations, prior to subject
dispensing. Sites had no additional checkpoints preventing dispensing of the investigative drug
product after initial assignment by interactive web response systems. For example, the audit
(b) (6)
found that the investigational drug product was administered to Subject
(treatment arm
not specified) after undergoing a temperature deviation at the clinical site. However, no
consequential adverse events were reported following temperature excursions, and (b) Complete
query resolution and clarifications between affiliates (that is, affiliate study managers and

monitors) and clinical investigators should be documented allowing for appropriate handling and
follow-up. For example, clarification received from a clinical investigator, for the timely
reporting of the onset dates of a serious adverse event via e-mail, was not captured in the queries
or updated in the electronic data capture data. In general, the Applicant’s oversight and
monitoring of this clinical study appear to be acceptable.
Dr. Orencia concludes in his review that based on the inspections, the study appears to have been

recommended at this time for Study 4311.
